# The Ten Toes Down Panorama of Games for Lillian - The Tellings Cut

ChatGPT, under Nolan’s creative direction

The Tellings Cut 2.0 / 2026-09-20


## Read this when you want to choose a game

The Tellings Cut is an adult comic edition with reclaimed in-group language, strong profanity, cocaine references and sexual innuendo. The practical game information stays in plain language.

On each card: read the short pitch, what you do, controls and pace, main catch, and first try. Aiden’s scene follows the useful information. Use the table of contents to jump directly to a game.

Volume 1: mysteries and adventures. Volume 2: strategy and role-playing. Volume 3: exploration. Volume 4: puzzles and building. Volume 5: character stories. Volume 6: co-op and shared play.

A place to start is a promising candidate, read the catch first flags a meaningful obstacle, and a detour is a narrower or more demanding option. These are editorial labels, not guarantees. Choose by interest and the stated catch.


## The Cocaine Was Not Included in the Rental

“Nigga, who put cocaine on the fucking instruction manual?”

Nobody answered.

Six televisions. Two hundred and forty games. A champagne tower tall enough to require its own evacuation plan. I had rented the private floor of a casino because Lillian might enjoy a game, and apparently my friends had understood that to mean the building was now a country with no laws.

Tank leaned over the manual.

“That yours?”

“The manual?”

“You know which fucking thing, nigga.”

“No.”

“Then why you defending the table?”

The man who owned the cocaine came out of the bathroom buttoning the wrong shirt. His name was Darnell. I knew it was the wrong shirt because mine was blue, embroidered with my initials, and no longer hanging where I had put it.

“Aiden, my nigga. This party is incredible.”

“You been here eleven minutes.”

“Best eleven of my life.”

Mercedes came out behind him carrying her own jacket. “He rounded up.”

The room went quiet for exactly as long as it took Tank to swallow.

“Damn.”

“Nothing happened,” she said. “He spent the whole time explaining his boat.”

“It’s a yacht,” Darnell said.

“Then explain it to the water.”

She took her drink from the piano and sat down beside Porsche. Darnell looked at me like a man requesting international aid. Nigga, I had six televisions to connect. His maritime emergency was going to have to wait.

Shawna came through the door in a black dress with a slit that made me forget three passwords. She looked at the champagne, the cables, the casino employee holding my credit card with both hands.

“You said a little game night.”

“For us, this is little.”

“You hired an announcer.”

From the next room: “LADIES AND GENTLEMEN—”

“Nigga, break,” I called.

The microphone clicked off.

My wife put a hand on my chest. “I thought we were having a quiet evening.”

“We still can.”

“You installed a bar in the quiet evening.”

“So we don’t have to leave it.”

She laughed despite herself. That was my opening. I kissed her, and for half a second the whole expensive fucking floor could have fallen into the desert. I had the important part exactly where I wanted it.

Then Tank dropped a speaker.

“Good bass,” he said from behind the couch.

Shawna kept her forehead against mine. “Fix your party. Then come find me.”

“That a promise?”

“That’s the order of operations.”

She took my champagne and went to help Rosa clear a table. I watched her go until Porsche waved a cable in front of my face.

“Nigga. HDMI.”

“I’m married.”

“Congratulations. Plug this in.”

Dre arrived wearing a blue tracksuit and carrying a casserole. Dre was a Crip, an excellent cook, and the only person in the room who had read the parking restrictions. He put the dish down and looked at the controller colors.

“Blue one’s mine.”

“Because—” I started.

“Because I fucking called it, Aiden.”

He went to get a plate. Tank followed him like the casserole had a second act.

That left me with the employee, the credit card and six rooms full of games. Mysteries. Stories. Worlds to wander through. Things to build. Turns nobody had to hurry. Ways to play together, or sit beside somebody and argue about who murdered the rich asshole.

Porsche looked up from the first screen. “Rich asshole’s usually got it coming.”

“I am standing right here.”

“We know. That’s why we haven’t started the mystery.”

The employee cleared his throat. He wanted a signature for the damage deposit.

“Nothing’s damaged,” I said.

Behind the couch, Tank said, “Define speaker.”

I signed.

So, Lillian. Here’s the Panorama. I brought the games. My niggas brought every other fucking problem. You can use the ridiculous dice, choose straight from the index, read the stories, or tell the whole production to move because you want that seat. Nobody has bought your evening just because I have mortgaged mine.

The man in my shirt reached for the manual again. Rosa picked it up before he could.

“This is the game table.”

“Where do I—”

“Not a question for me.”

He left with his belongings. She gave the cleared manual to Porsche, who actually needed it. We had achieved one useful thing, and it had cost me almost nothing.

Then the announcer asked about overtime.


## You Have the Controller. Aiden Has a Bill.

There is a direct index in every volume. You can pick a title and read its ordinary description without rolling anything. You can also follow the full ceremonial route, invent a ridiculous qualification, appeal to a committee and arrive at the same title with considerably more paperwork. Both are valid ways to use this book.

No rule can require a game you do not want. No fictional point total can overrule uncomfortable controls, a disliked theme, a wish to stop, or the fact that a game is not available on the system you own. “No” is a complete answer and costs no tokens. Aiden can survive disappointment. Shawna has receipts.

The collection has a range of demands. Promising entries are plausible starting points. Conditional entries have a meaningful obstacle that needs checking. Detours are here for a narrower activity or a different appetite; they are not quietly being passed off as universally easy. Some turn-based games have enormous menus. Some gentle-looking games have timed sequences. The catches are part of the recommendation.

A card’s first try is an invitation to sample one action, not a prescribed duration or a performance test. Ask before taking over the controls. Sharing a theory, reading dialogue aloud, playing a second character and passing a controller are different kinds of company. Choose the one that feels welcome.

The source desk records what was actually supported by a source. A Steam accessibility declaration is a developer declaration, not a personal guarantee. A blank or unknown control detail remains unknown. No installed-game trial or complete-game accessibility audit is claimed. This book is for leisure, not treatment.

All the research still leaves the useful question to you: do you like it? “Nigga, she picked something,” Tank said while I was preparing the welcome speech. I looked over. He was right. Porsche unplugged the microphone before I could improve the moment.


## The Rules Department Has Acquired a Rules Department

### Article 1. Enter through any door you like

Choose a department by what you want to do. Book I investigates suspicious people. Book II lets a turn wait for you. Book III looks around. Book IV moves ideas and objects into place. Book V listens to characters. Book VI brings company. A game belongs to one shelf in this Panorama so the count stays honest; its pleasures can overlap several shelves.

I wanted a seventh room just for introductions. “Nigga, introduce yourself to this invoice,” Shawna said. The casino charged by the room. I discovered the artistic power of six.

For the practical route, choose two titles from a shelf, read the catch, and try the more appealing opening. For the ceremonial route, continue below. The ceremony does not know better than you do.

### Article 2. Name what the evening needs

Write or say up to three actual preferences: no required rush; no separate camera steering; avoid held input; few menus; light reading; little clue tracking; or native co-op. Add any theme you do not want. You can choose fewer than three or add your own.

These are boundaries for the selection, not traits assigned to a person. They can change next time. The record should read “tonight, no camera wrestling,” not an assessment of somebody’s capabilities. One is useful information. The other is a stranger wearing a clipboard.

A source gap is not permission to promise a feature. If we have not established how a game handles a requirement, its card is a question to check or a reason to choose another game. The optional web finder treats strict feature filters conservatively. The printed cards put the concrete catch beside the temptation.

“What if I already paid for it?” I asked.

“Then you own something she doesn’t want to play,” Shawna said.

“Nigga, that’s brutal.”

“That’s shopping.”

### Article 3. Choose a row, because the binder demands coordinates

Each department has forty cases. Pick one of five rows: 1, Quietly Curious; 2, Ready for a Conversation; 3, Willing to Inspect a Thing; 4, Here for the Atmosphere; 5, Open to a Wildcard. These are ceremonial mood names. They do not certify that every game on a row meets that mood; the actual card and preferences decide that.

Choose a row freely. If you want dice, roll one ordinary six-sided die. Results 1–5 select that row. On a 6, visit the Incident Desk once and then choose a row yourself. There is no recursive reroll trap. The Incident Desk has had its authority to extend the evening revoked.

I picked Ready for Conversation. Mercedes said, “With who?” “The room.” “The room’s trying to have sex, Aiden. Pick Quietly Curious and mind your business.” I picked Here for the Atmosphere. She let me have that.

### Article 4. Select the object whose paperwork you distrust

Choose an emblem, or roll an eight-sided die: 1 Envelope, 2 Key, 3 Plant, 4 Map, 5 Teacup, 6 Spare Chair, 7 Gold Stamp, 8 Clock. Read the corresponding column of the department’s five-by-eight route grid.

The grid gives a case number. For example, department 1, row 2, emblem 3 gives local case 11, P011. Department 6 at those same coordinates gives P211. The same coordinates in different departments lead to different games. This is either a filing system or a multiverse, depending on whether you are speaking to Shawna or me.

The forty cases stay in catalogue order, not easiest-to-hardest order. The emblem just chooses where to look. “Why the fuck would a teacup know my future?” Tank asked. “You asked that casserole for directions ten minutes ago,” Rosa said. “And I’m happy with where I ended up.”

### Article 5. Optional: give the evening an outrageously specific title

Write one short session trait. Examples: “Two Detectives, One Sandwich”; “The Map Can Come to Me”; “I Want to Know What She Meant by That”; “A House with Excellent Stopping Places”; “The Plant Is Lying”; “My Friend Has the Other Controller.” A trait describes a taste or a situation, not a diagnosis.

Whenever it helps explain why a title sounds interesting, point to it. That explanation is already useful. You do not need a numeric bonus to care about a haunted town or a witness with a suspicious alibi.

If you enjoy the machinery, begin with three imaginary Stamp Tokens. Spend one to add two to the paperwork total OR reroll all four paperwork dice once. At most one token per selection. Refusing costs nothing; tokens never buy exceptions to preferences. “Are these casino chips?” Tank asked. “No money attached.” “Nigga, you finally invented something affordable.”

### Article 6. Four dice that cannot be cashed at the bar

Roll four ordinary six-sided dice. Each 1 or 2 means minus one, each 3 or 4 means zero, and each 5 or 6 means plus one. Add the four values. Your paperwork total is between minus four and plus four. This optional layer draws on Fate’s familiar signed-dice idea; the surrounding selection rules are original Panorama rules.

A negative result moves backward around the forty-case shelf; a positive result moves forward. Zero keeps the same case. “Nigga, I rolled four dice to stay here?” Tank asked. “You were happy here.” “I was happy before the fucking dice.”

If you spend a Stamp Token, either add two or reroll all four dice once and use the new total. You cannot buy a second modification on this selection. Reset to three tokens whenever you begin a new browsing session; no ledger needs to survive dinner.

The dice can produce a total from minus four to plus four. A +2 stamp can raise that part to plus six; the separate refreshment adjustment can add another zero, one or two. “Why four dice?” Shawna asked. I said it gave the evening texture. “So does carpet, nigga. You don’t roll the fucking carpet.”

### Article 7. Optional: the snack amendment

Record a separate snack adjustment: one if somebody has already brought the drink they wanted, plus one if wanted snacks are within reach and no one is expected to fetch them. The maximum snack adjustment is two. Keep it separate from the dice-and-stamp paperwork total; Article 8 adds it exactly once. If these conditions are irrelevant, use zero. This arithmetic is a joke about hospitality; nobody needs to eat or drink anything to participate.

Tank asked whether Dre’s casserole counted twice. Rosa said no. “Nigga, it got layers.” She put a fork in his hand. His appeal ended when he remembered what forks did.

Do not delay choosing a game to qualify for this amendment. Do not make a snack score into a task for somebody else. The best possible snack state is people having what they actually want.

### Article 8. The entirely unnecessary formula

First find the local case from the grid: eight times (row minus one), plus emblem. Add the paperwork total and the snack adjustment, if you used them. If the result exceeds forty, subtract forty until it is 1–40. If it is below one, add forty until it is 1–40. Otherwise keep it. The final local case number is always between 1 and 40.

Then add forty times (department minus one) to get the complete P-number. Department 3, row 1, emblem 2 starts at local case 2. A paperwork total of minus three and a snack adjustment of one gives zero. Wrap zero to forty. In department 3, local case forty is P120.

This is a shelf address. It is not a probability of enjoyment, a cognitive score, a ranking, or an instruction to buy something. It chooses a page to consider. Read that page’s ordinary fields before treating it as a candidate.

If arithmetic gets in the way, use the offline finder or choose directly. “I’m not doing long division to sit with my wife,” Tank said. Rosa slid over. The system had encountered a man with better navigation.

### Article 9. Read the game first; Aiden can wait

Read the short pitch, what you do, main catch and first try before the longer comic scene. The full appeal and catch give the detail behind those summaries. Aiden’s scene and verdict are fiction; a funny reason is not evidence for a game feature.

Check the exact edition and platform. A remake can change movement, menus and saves. A touch port can differ from a desktop pointer. A Steam store date may be the port’s date rather than the original release. Local co-op, online co-op and two people discussing a single-player game are not interchangeable.

If the card conflicts with a real preference, reject the candidate. If it is promising but has an unresolved operation, check that specific operation before investing in it. “Can we select this inventory object comfortably?” is more useful than “Is this an easy game?”

I called the result destiny. Shawna asked why destiny needed my credit card. “For incidentals.” “Nigga, you’re the incidental.” She went back to the game. I tried to make the stamp hit the page quietly.

### Article 10. The appeals court

You may make one ceremonial appeal or skip the ceremony entirely. To appeal, choose a neighbor: the previous local case, the next local case, or the same local case in another department. Wrap case 1 backward to 40 and case 40 forward to 1. Check the new card as usual.

After that one appeal, stop calculating. Choose either candidate, browse the direct index or close the book. Every route ends with a human decision; there is no compulsory loop, escalating fee or penalty for declining both.

“I appeal,” I said. “On what grounds?” Rosa asked. “Everybody’s having fun without my explanation.” “Denied, nigga.” She didn’t even look away from the screen. The court had excellent instincts.

If you would rather talk, talk. If you would rather stop, stop. “What if we want to go upstairs?” Shawna asked. I put down the stamp. Nigga, some appeals deserve immediate personal attention.

### Article 11. A genuinely useful first session

Load the chosen edition before introducing its controls. Show one meaningful action. Keep a reminder if wanted. Find pause, save or exit so leaving feels ordinary. Do not turn a trial into a lesson with a hidden passing grade.

For a mystery, that might mean one conversation or one clue. For an exploration game, a nearby path and a place to stop. For a turn-based game, one decision with the menus already explained. For co-op, agree who wants which role and whether the roles have equal kinds of action.

Stop, change games or take a break whenever wanted. A day off makes room for leisure; it does not require a whole day of screen time. The useful note afterward is a sentence about what was enjoyable and where to resume.

“How will we know it worked?” I asked.

Mercedes pointed at the couch. Two people were arguing cheerfully about a clue. Neither had looked at me for ten minutes.

“That,” she said.

“Nigga, I could have stayed home.”

“We would have missed the deposit.”

### Article 12. What these departments are allowed to claim

This is a researched, source-linked candidate catalogue with original comic writing. Game descriptions were checked against publisher, developer, platform or identified supporting sources. Control classifications and first-trial suggestions are editorial judgments unless tied to an explicit declaration. No card claims a complete-path playtest.

The catalogue deliberately contains different levels of complexity. Turn-based strategy and elaborate deduction belong here only for readers who want those specific demands. The direct index and catches let you pass them by. “There are two hundred and forty games” does not mean “all two hundred and forty will suit the same evening.”

The offline finder uses the same case IDs as the books. It makes no network requests and does not inspect your library. Marking a title interesting does not install or buy it. Its strict feature filters require stated evidence; an unknown is not silently accepted.

The final authority is the person playing. I told Tank I could make any game irresistible. “Then make me want this fishing minigame, nigga.” I offered him a hundred dollars. He held out his hand without picking up the controller. That was the end of my theory.


## Twelve optional declarations of personality

1. Two Detectives, One Suspicious Motherfucker. Mysteries to discuss together. Shared theorizing is useful company; it is not native co-op.

2. Nigga, Bring the Map Over Here. Prefer a desk, static scenes or fixed framing; inspect the actual navigation demands.

3. The Ghost Still Owes Me Money. Supernatural investigations; check horror and emotional tone as well as controls.

4. Penthouse Problems, One-Room Budget. A compact place can offer interesting people. Small worlds and simple decisions are different claims.

5. My Turn Is None of Your Fucking Business. Turn-based candidates may wait while you think. Check the menus and planning demands separately.

6. You Drive. I Have Opinions.. Agree on the kind of company wanted: native co-op, passing the controller or talking through a single-player game.

7. That Plant Knows Too Much. Observation and occult mysteries; check the cross-referencing burden.

8. I Bought the House. Where Does This Wall Go?. Creative modes and construction; confirm that a quiet mode exists in the exact edition.

9. I Came for the Gossip. Character stories over equipment management. Reading load and choice pressure still matter.

10. A Whole City and No Appointment. Exploration with a strong sense of place; camera and navigation need their own check.

11. The Puzzle Can Wait. I Look Good.. Look for stated untimed behavior. A flattering title cannot establish a feature.

12. Aiden Can Kiss My Entire Agenda. Choose directly. This trait defeats every arbitrary rule in this book without rolling anything.


## The incident desk

1. Cocaine on the wrong paperwork. Darnell mistakes the evening for an afterparty again. Rosa clears the game table. No fictional drug changes your roll; choose a row while Aiden negotiates the cleaning bill.

2. The blue controller has been claimed. Dre calls dibs. Aiden tries to make a speech about Crip diplomacy. “Nigga, I called dibs,” Dre says. Choose a row; no controller color changes a game feature.

3. The bedroom is a different department. Shawna takes Aiden by the collar. Tank is appointed to say “choose a row” while the chairman discovers a shorter agenda.

4. The champagne tower has opinions. Porsche moves one glass. Aiden moves six people. Nothing falls, but everybody learns how high his voice goes. Choose your row.

5. Mercedes has heard about the yacht. Darnell begins the story again. “Nigga, I hope that boat finds somebody,” she says. Choose a row before the measurements start.

6. Tank thought the chips were edible. He knew they were tokens. He wanted everybody to remember they had not ordered fries. Choose your row; the kitchen argument grants no bonus.

7. Aiden appears in the wrong trailer. The projector shows his karaoke rehearsal instead of the game. Shawna keeps the remote. Choose a row while he attempts copyright enforcement.

8. The hotel sends the bill. The employee puts down a second page. Aiden turns it over. There is a third. Choose your row while wealth has a private moment.

9. Somebody finds the missing heel. It is beside the subwoofer. Three adults deny knowing why. Nobody asks a follow-up because the game has finally loaded. Choose your row.

10. Rosa has already won. She has her payment, a plate and the good seat. “Nigga, that’s strategy,” Tank says. Choose a row; her success does not certify any game as easy.

11. A very expensive entrance. Aiden enters through the wrong door and comes back holding a mop. Everybody claps. Choose your row while he explains the symbolism.

12. The game is better than the speech. Porsche turns the microphone off. Aiden hears everybody laugh at something on the screen. Choose a row, or join them. Nobody owes him an encore.


## Volume 1: Forty Mysteries and a Hotel That Knows My Name

Mystery, investigation and classic adventures


## The Deposit Was Supposed to Be Refundable

“Nigga, why does the sign say intimate?” Tank asked. Behind him, two men were assembling a doorway large enough to admit a modest yacht. A third man held my face. Six feet of it, printed on a board. The face looked confident. That nigga had not seen the invoice.

“Because it's a gathering of close friends.” Tank looked at the ballroom. “How close? We sharing a zip code?” Shawna walked between us with her evening shoes in one hand. She had booked a suite, chosen a dress, and asked for a night where she could actually enjoy her husband. I had interpreted this as a request for three adjoining entertainment spaces and a photographer. My wife loved me deeply. Love sometimes looked like a woman stopping in a doorway to count backward.

The casino hotel called the room Imperial. I had called the party Panorama. Mercedes called it a soundcheck waiting to be interrupted. She was doing a short live set because she wanted to try an arrangement with musicians she trusted. She wanted a recording she controlled, a working monitor, and nobody from the drinks sponsor explaining how to reach a younger audience. Everyone in our party was a grown adult. Several of us had grown much more successfully than I had.

Rosa arrived with the catering supervisor and put a hand against the decorative doorway. “Food goes through here?” I said guests would pass beneath it and feel transported. She asked whether transported guests intended to eat. The doorway moved. Tank helped move it, then came back holding a tiny gold torch from the decoration crate. “What this belong to?” I pointed at a cloth-covered statue. He lifted one corner, stared, and let the cloth fall. “Nigga, we discussing that later.”

I had wanted an evening with games because somebody had asked what might be enjoyable. Mysteries. Interesting people. Places where a person could stop and think. I understood all of that. Unfortunately I also understood champagne towers, hired atmosphere, and the magnificent possibilities of being photographed pointing at something. The people who loved me had arrived to enjoy the first idea and survive the rest.

Shawna slipped her arm through mine. “You remember upstairs?” “Baby, I remember everything.” “You remember the strawberries?” I looked toward the buffet. Her fingers tightened. “The private strawberries, Aiden.” Oh. Those strawberries. I leaned closer and told her I had reserved considerable personal attention for her. She said attention had been reserved all afternoon and was beginning to incur a cancellation fee.

Mercedes came over carrying a black equipment bag. “This stays with me or Porsche. The recorder and spare drive are in here.” I reached to take it. She moved it back. “Are you me or Porsche?” “I'm offering support.” “Offer your elbow to your wife. She looks under-supported.” Shawna accepted the elbow with a smile that made my hotel trousers feel like an ambitious tailoring decision.

Tank's old friend Darnell arrived in a blue jacket and announced that he'd brought enough atmosphere for everybody. Tank looked at the bag, then looked at him. “My nigga, tell me you mean playlists.” Darnell smiled instead of answering. Mercedes pointed him toward the coat room, away from her equipment. We would be learning more about his contribution shortly, which is usually how a sentence about Darnell develops.

The photographer asked me to stand beside the sponsor's banner. I did. The sponsor asked Mercedes to join me holding a bottle. She said she'd be happy to discuss a separate advertising fee. He laughed. She didn't. The photographer lowered his camera and became interested in a light stand. A whole room of professionals suddenly understood that my friend was working, and I was the only nigga dressed like a man receiving a lifetime achievement award for booking chairs.

Shawna took the microphone before I could introduce the evening. “The games are in there. Pick what interests you. The practical notes explain what each one asks. Nobody has to finish anything.” Short. Clear. Completely devastating to my twelve-minute welcome. I asked whether I could add a thought. “At dinner,” she said. “To one person.” Tank put a plate in my free hand. Rosa pointed at the room. Mercedes began testing her microphone. The party started without waiting for my permission, and my face on the big board watched it happen.


## P001 - The Blackwell Legacy

### In one sentence

Meet a reluctant New York medium and her sharp-tongued ghost partner in a compact mystery built around their developing relationship.

### What you do

Talk with people, follow notebook leads, and connect names or clues while revisiting places in a supernatural investigation.

### How you play

Mouse / pointer | Usually waits for choices | Single-player; a friend can share the decisions

### Main catch

Names and notebook combinations take tracking; verify manual speech advance, and consider the suicide and bereavement themes.

### Edition considered

PC original game; current publisher patch must be recorded

### Editorial starting point

P001  /  A PLACE TO START

### Why it might click

A compact New York supernatural mystery joins a reluctant medium to a sardonic spirit guide. Conversations and notebook connections make discovery feel personal; the developing partnership offers more reason to continue than simply clearing another puzzle.

### The full catch

Notebook combinations, returning to locations, and remembering names remain real work. Suicide and bereavement are central. Reviews report no timed or dexterity challenges, but this is not a complete-path playtest. Current desktop patches offer manual speech advance through a Ctrl+P toggle; verify it waits.

### Try this first

Set manual speech advance if you want it, with help if useful. Try one conversation and one notebook lead, then decide whether these two people are company worth keeping.

### Aiden after hours

The Ghost Doesn't Pay the Resort Fee

I put a second chair beside the screen for Joey Mallone. Blackwell had a woman dealing with a ghost; my hotel had charged me for six hundred people, and I intended to count the dead.

Tank picked up the place card. “Nigga, this seat got a meal?”

“Spiritually.” “Then move your spiritual ass. Rosa made crab cakes.” He sat down and ate Joey's welcome portion before I could establish contact.

Our Rosa watched the game's Rosa talk to somebody nobody else could see. “I like her,” she said. “People keep giving this woman responsibilities she didn't order.” She looked directly at me. I checked behind myself. Nothing there. Expensive habit.

Shawna leaned against my shoulder. “A ghost could walk through our suite door.” “Baby, so could I.” “Last night you walked into it.”

Joey had been dead for years. Nigga still entered a room with more dignity than me.

### Aiden’s verdict

Try the partnership if it catches you; Joey can handle supernatural business while I handle his crab-cake debt, which Tank insists survives death.

### Evidence desk

Inspected publisher premise and developer Ctrl+P patch notice; timing description is a secondary review, not current complete-path testing. PC original only.

## P002 - The Darkside Detective

### In one sentence

Investigate small, absurd paranormal cases with a recurring comic detective partnership, using static scenes that keep the focus on dialogue and objects.

### What you do

Inspect objects, read conversations, and try using inventory items on the scene or on one another to advance a case.

### How you play

Mouse / pointer | Usually waits for choices | Single-player; a friend can share the decisions

### Main catch

Inventory dragging, small hotspots, and pipe or tile puzzles need checking even though timed input is not declared.

### Edition considered

PC first game; current accessibility build must be recorded

### Editorial starting point

P002  /  READ THE CATCH FIRST

### Why it might click

Comic paranormal investigations offer a recurring detective partnership and short case boundaries. Static scenes let attention settle on objects, dialogue, and absurd situations rather than keeping an avatar and camera moving together.

### The full catch

Current Steam metadata declares play without timed input. That does not mean hold-free: PC reviews describe dragging inventory items onto objects or other items. Pipe and tile puzzles also appear. Text reading and small hotspots deserve an actual check before treating this as comfortable.

### Try this first

Begin the first case, inspect one object, and test one inventory drag. Check that text advance and the save/exit route feel understandable before continuing.

### Aiden after hours

The Paranormal Department Has My Room Key

Somebody called the ballroom phone and breathed into it. I told security we had a haunting. Mercedes took the receiver, listened, and said, “Tank. Stop eating against the microphone.”

“Nigga, I'm ordering ice.” “You're calling the stage.” “Then send some dramatic ice.”

The Darkside Detective appeared on the monitor while the hotel manager asked why I had requested an exorcist. I pointed at McQueen. “Professional comparison.” He pointed at the charge for an emergency callout. The hotel apparently billed ghosts by the hour.

Shawna tested an inventory movement. I reached over to demonstrate and dragged the wrong thing. She rested her hand on mine. “Help me by stopping.” That felt almost sexual until she moved my whole arm off the table.

Mercedes gave Tank the correct number. He ordered ice, a sandwich, and cancellation of the priest. The sandwich arrived first. Stronger management than mine.

### Aiden’s verdict

The little cases look inviting; test the actual dragging before settling in, and don't let a loud husband classify every ordinary inconvenience as supernatural.

### Evidence desk

Primary Steam accessibility declarations inspected. Inventory dragging is documented by a PC review; no hold-free alternative was verified.

## P003 - Phoenix Wright: Ace Attorney Trilogy

### In one sentence

Meet theatrical witnesses and recurring friends in courtroom mysteries where matching a statement to evidence can produce a satisfying breakthrough.

### What you do

Read testimony, consult the Court Record, and compare statements with evidence; the updated compilation also offers Story Mode assistance.

### How you play

Mouse / pointer | Usually waits for choices | Single-player; a friend can share the decisions

### Main catch

Reading and evidence menus are central; automatic text progression may feel rushed, and wrong evidence can incur penalties.

### Edition considered

2019 PC compilation; post-November 2025 update scope

### Editorial starting point

P003  /  A PLACE TO START

### Why it might click

The original trilogy supplies memorable witnesses, recurring friendships, and theatrical courtroom mysteries. Comparing testimony with a piece of evidence can deliver a satisfying click of understanding; the story has room for comedy and emotional stakes.

### The full catch

Reading and Court Record menus are central, and wrong evidence can incur penalties. Dramatic music may feel urgent even when the interface waits. The November 2025 update adds Story Mode and Auto-play; automatic text progression may itself feel rushed. Later evidence interactions still need checking.

### Try this first

Use the updated 2019 compilation. Start The First Turnabout with manual text advance, compare one statement, and use Story Mode whenever enjoying the next scene matters more than solving it.

### Aiden after hours

Objection, That Is My Wife's Room Charge

I slapped the room-service receipt beside Phoenix Wright. “Somebody charged strawberries and champagne to my suite.”

Shawna put down her glass. “I did.” “For whom?” “The man I married. He was supposed to come upstairs an hour ago.”

Tank leaned across the screen. “Nigga, prosecute yourself.” Mercedes made a little courtroom noise with her wineglass. Everybody suddenly loved the justice system.

I tried to compare a witness statement with the Court Record, but Shawna kept moving the receipt into my field of vision. “You promised a private celebration.” “I'm building suspense.” “You're building a service charge.”

Onscreen, a contradiction finally clicked for me. In the room, three women had already established one without needing a walkthrough. I stood, offered Shawna my arm, and knocked the receipt into Tank's sauce.

“Evidence tampering,” he said. Nigga licked the corner. Chain of custody was fucked.

### Aiden’s verdict

Follow the courtroom drama at your own pace and use the available story help freely; my actual marriage has declined to install a skip-cross-examination button.

### Aiden after hours

Who Put a Gold Dick on the Dessert Table?

The hotel wheeled in my commissioned golden idol. It was supposed to be a triumphant figure holding a torch. From the dessert table it looked like a man having an expensive medical problem.

“Nigga,” Tank said, “why your statue hard?” “That's the torch.” “Then the nigga needs a smaller fire.”

Mercedes turned it toward the wall. Shawna turned it back because that exposed the rear view. Rosa put a cake in front of it and charged me for the cake.

The Case of the Golden Idol offered a crime scene where details actually mattered. I studied the people and objects. Behind me, my friends reconstructed who had approved the sculpture, who had ignored the sketch, and who had paid for overnight delivery.

Every clue led to me. I asked if we could leave the conclusion unfinished. Shawna put the invoice beneath the statue. Now it had both motive and evidence.

## The Break Room Had Cocaine in It for About Thirty Seconds

Darnell opened his bag in the little room behind the stage. Porsche looked down, looked up, and closed the door before a hotel employee could wander into his explanation. “You said you brought atmosphere.” “I did.” She pointed at the bag of cocaine. “Nigga, that's a different department.”

I arrived because I heard voices and assumed my authority was needed. Mercedes arrived because she needed her spare cable. This gave her a useful purpose and me a terrible disadvantage. Darnell wanted us to understand that he wasn't creating a problem. He had brought his own problem, personally, at considerable expense. Tank stood in the doorway. “Then take it somewhere that ain't my friend's workplace.”

Nobody delivered a speech. Mercedes held out her hand for the cable bag, which looked much too much like Darnell's. Porsche gave her the correct one. Darnell took his bag away. I started saying something about the changing character of nightlife. Shawna appeared behind me. “You hired a gold statue with its dick out.” “That's a torch.” Tank sighed. “We looked again, my nigga. Architecturally, you're alone.”

In the ballroom, Rosa was solving a more expensive emergency. The sponsor had promised extra guests without promising extra money. Four adults stood beside an empty table, watching the staff try to create dinner out of politeness. Rosa told them there was plenty of food once someone approved the adjustment. All four looked at me with the soft, hopeful faces people wear near a man who has put his own portrait on the wall.

I paid. Rosa checked the confirmation. Then she fed them. The sequence had a beauty no speech of mine could improve, so naturally I tried anyway. “A host must always—” “Move your elbow,” she said. Hot dishes went past me. I moved my elbow. History would record my contribution accurately if everybody could resist exaggerating it.

Shawna pulled me into the quiet side room and shut the door. I smiled. She smiled back. Then she handed me my jacket, which had been lying across a speaker. “I know that face,” she said. “You think every closed door is a proposal.” “Baby, with you it could be.” She kissed me slowly enough to make the room service bill seem reasonable. Then somebody knocked. Three knocks. Tank. “Your statue is blocking dessert.”

“Let it block dessert,” I said. The silence outside was immediate and terrible. Even Shawna stopped kissing me. Nigga, I had threatened cake in a room full of people who had survived my welcome speech. She opened the door and sent me back into public life with my jacket straight and my priorities under review.

I found Mercedes moving the gold figure herself. I took the heavy end. She let me, then told me exactly where she wanted it. Together we cleared the sightline to the stage. She didn't thank me for rescuing her from an object I'd ordered. Fair. Tank rolled the dessert trolley through and gave us both a plate. For several minutes I carried something useful, stayed out of an argument, and ate cake beside my friends. I considered announcing this improvement. Shawna touched my wrist. I let the achievement remain unofficial.

Time Travel Does Not Reverse the Minibar

Old Skies gave me a time-travel idea. “We return to yesterday,” I told Shawna, “and book the smaller ballroom.”

“You return to yesterday and listen when I suggest it.” Nigga, history had a witness.

Tank arrived carrying two identical black bags. One contained Mercedes's spare cables. The other belonged to Darnell, an adult guest who'd announced he was bringing “a little atmosphere.” Mercedes opened it far enough to see a bag of cocaine and closed it again.

Check the PC controls in practice; do not import PlayStation button instructions or assume remaster means simplified puzzles.

### Try this first

Explore Manny's first available workplace interactions, compare the installed control choices, and test movement between two nearby points. Let the voice and atmosphere win your interest before agreeing to carry a long inventory of unsolved errands.

### Aiden after hours

The Afterlife Has an Upgrade Desk

Grim Fandango gave us travel agents for the dead. I immediately recognized the hotel manager's business model. “You'd sell a deceased nigga the premium package,” I told him.

“Only if he requested a late checkout.” Shawna laughed before I could defend the living.

Tank wanted to know whether the best afterlife transport included dinner. Rosa said she wasn't catering eternity without a deposit. Mercedes liked the style enough to sit closer to the screen, which seemed a more sensible response than my negotiations with death.

I offered Shawna a romantic journey beyond mortality. “Can we start with the lift upstairs?” she asked. “I have shoes to change.”

I escorted her without an announcement. At the lift, the manager asked whether we needed anything else. I said no.

Nigga looked shocked. Death, rebirth, a skeleton selling journeys—none of it had prepared him for a client refusing an optional charge.

### Aiden’s verdict

The underworld's characters and style may make the old puzzles worthwhile to you; my romantic itinerary now begins with letting my wife take off her heels.

### Aiden after hours

The Clown Is Not on My Payroll

Thimbleweed Park put strange people in a strange town. Our party put an adult clown beside the casino cashier. I hadn't booked him, which made him the cheapest entertainment I'd seen all night.

He had followed the wrong event sign. Tank invited him to eat before he left. Mercedes asked him to keep the horn away from soundcheck. He agreed more readily than most sponsors.

I watched the game's cast and said every ensemble needed one unpredictable personality. Shawna looked at the clown, then me, then back at the clown. “We already had coverage.”

He asked where his actual party was. Rosa checked the booking text and sent him two floors down with a wrapped slice of cake.

At the lift he honked once at me. I honked my car key in reply. The valet alarm went off outside. The clown bowed.

Nigga knew when he'd met a professional and when to let him embarrass himself.

### Aiden after hours

The Biker Wants the Loading Dock Cleared

Full Throttle went onto the screen when the motorcycle performer I'd forgotten booking arrived at the loading dock. He wanted to know where the stunt area was.

“What stunt area?” Shawna asked. I suddenly remembered approving “maximum impact.”

Mercedes stood beside the cable cases. “Nigga, maximum impact is what happens when that bike hits my equipment.” The performer looked at the space and refused the stunt himself. Competence was everywhere except my signature.

Tank helped him park outside. Rosa offered him dinner. I paid his callout and accepted a photograph beside the stationary bike as our evening's remaining speed event.

The game's action was a real part of its appeal and its catch. We left both plainly visible instead of pretending a biker adventure could be made entirely still by enthusiasm.

Shawna mounted the parked bike behind me for the picture. “Hold on,” I said. “We're not moving.” “Baby, your ego corners hard.”

## The Singer Got the Stage and I Got the Bill

By the time Mercedes was ready to sing, my portrait had moved, the motorcycle was outside, the fog machine had gone home unpaid in glory but paid in cash, and the mermaids were wearing shoes. Two guests were still playing in the quiet room. Nobody hauled them out for a mandatory appreciation of Aiden. I had started the evening believing a successful party required everyone looking in the same direction. Now I was pleased that the direction was only occasionally the exit.

The sponsor came to me holding a revised introduction. He had restored his product name to the first sentence. I looked at it, looked at Mercedes checking her microphone, and handed it back. “Use the one she agreed to.” He said this was a commercial relationship. “Then listen to the professional you hired.” He waited for my usual nervous little improvement, the sentence where I'd promise him something expensive to avoid silence. I let him wait. Nigga, apparently you could do that outside a game too.

Shawna stood beside the sound desk. Tank and Rosa found chairs together. Porsche held the spare recording bag on her lap because she'd said she would. The band began. Mercedes heard the first chord, turned her head a little, and smiled at the pianist. It wasn't a smile for the sponsor or the camera. It belonged to a moment between people making something together. I put my phone away before I tried to own it.

She sang. No interruption, no surprise guest, no announcement from me about the enormous generosity involved in my silence. Halfway through, Tank leaned over and whispered, “My nigga, the bass.” I nodded. I could feel it in the chair. Shawna's hand found mine. We listened. The recording light stayed on, small and red, doing a job without projecting its face onto the wall.

Afterward Mercedes wanted to hear one particular passage before promising anybody a copy. The sponsor approached with his phone ready. She held up a finger and listened. He waited. I enjoyed that so much I nearly spoiled it by congratulating her while she was still listening. Porsche caught my eye and shook her head. A whole fucking support network existed to prevent my enthusiasm from becoming a second soundtrack.

The take was good enough for Mercedes to want it. That was the decision that mattered. She put the recorder away herself, hugged Rosa, and asked Tank whether there was anything left to eat. He looked offended on a structural level. There were covered plates in the back. He had secured them hours ago. Nigga had been running a quiet, competent nation behind my collapsing empire of atmosphere.

The manager arrived with the balance. I asked whether any of the deposit was coming back. He turned two pages. Shawna made a sympathetic noise that developed, against her better judgment, into laughter. The unused fog delivery, the relocated sculpture, the additional meals, the performers, the extra hour, my inexplicable request for monogrammed ice. On paper I had entertained a small principality. In practice my friends had eaten, talked, listened, and found games that made them curious. Most of the good parts fit on the cheapest page.

Shawna took my hand and led me toward the lift. “Now,” she said. I asked whether I could make one closing remark. She squeezed my hand. “Make it upstairs.” I had never accepted a scheduling instruction more enthusiastically. Tank called after us to ask what he should do with my portrait. “Keep it safe.” “Safe from who, nigga? Recognition?” Mercedes laughed. Rosa handed him a cloth. The doors closed before I saw what they covered.

Upstairs, the strawberries were still there. So was the champagne. Shawna took off her earrings and looked at me. “No microphones.” I put my phone facedown. “No audience.” I shut the curtain. “No twelve-minute introduction.” “Baby, I can be concise.” She came over, smiling. Downstairs I had needed three rooms to impress everybody. Here the only person I needed to impress was already pulling me away from the door. I let the rest of the evening keep its own record.


## Volume 2: The Emperor Has Lost His Security Deposit

Turn-based strategy, cards and role-playing


## They Charged Extra for the Throne

“Nigga, get out of that chair.” I had returned from the suite feeling loved, handsome, and capable of running a small civilization. Tank intended to challenge two of those conclusions immediately. He stood beside a large mechanical recliner covered in white upholstery. The rental company called it a photo throne. I called it a command center. Shawna called it the reason the service corridor was blocked.

I sat anyway. It made a low electrical noise. Somewhere beneath my ass a motor woke up and reconsidered the evening. Tank took one step back. Nigga had instincts honed by years of standing near my ideas. The chair remained upright for the moment. I took this as a sign of loyalty and put my feet on the little platform.

The second game room was supposed to be quieter. People could sit with strategic choices, stories, cards, and imaginary empires. I had therefore decorated it like the headquarters of a man who planned to invade another hotel. Two flags, a projection of stars, the throne, and a velvet rope around chairs that were exactly the same as the chairs outside the rope. The hotel manager looked in, saw me seated, and wrote something down. That nigga's pen had become a predator.

Shawna came over and kissed the top of my head. “We had a nice time upstairs.” “Exceptional,” I said. “Then preserve it by not becoming an emperor.” I told her I could rule gently. She looked at the foot platform. “You can't even get down without help.” I stood to demonstrate. The platform wobbled. Tank caught my elbow with the bored competence of a man who had already budgeted for this.

Mercedes was reviewing her live recording with Porsche. She wanted to hear the quiet parts without me announcing that the quiet parts were excellent. Rosa was settling the additional staff time. She wanted numbers to arrive in the right accounts instead of merely being praised for their importance. Tank wanted to sit, eat, and beat me at something sufficiently clear that I couldn't rewrite the result afterward. Everybody had an objective. Mine required the largest piece of furniture.

Dre came in carrying his casserole dish. A grown man with a blue shirt, an easy laugh, and no interest in auditioning for my themed militia. He was a Crip; this detail did not make him responsible for my blue lighting package. “That controller's mine,” he said, pointing at the blue one. I nodded. His claim was easier to understand than the rental agreement for my throne. Tank offered him a plate. A useful alliance formed without an anthem.

Darnell followed, talking about his yacht to somebody who was not following. He had already been told where his cocaine bag wasn't welcome. Now he wanted to explain that a luxury weekend should have freedom. Mercedes looked up. “Then free me from this conversation.” He laughed as though she was flirting. She wasn't. Porsche moved a chair into the gap between them and asked Mercedes a question about the recording. That was the end of Darnell's audience. The nigga wandered toward a bartender who at least got paid to remain polite.

I wanted to announce that turn-based games meant everybody could relax. Shawna stopped me before I reached the microphone. “Say what you actually know.” I knew some actions waited. I also knew menus could pile up, systems could demand memory, and a counter could make a person feel pressed even when no seconds were ticking away. Tank nodded toward my bills. “Like that. Your debt waits. Ain't relaxing.”

So the useful notes stayed plain. What looked appealing, what a person would do, what might be a pain in the ass. Nobody had to love a heavy strategy game merely because its battles politely waited to make them miserable. Equally, nobody had to avoid a large story if learning it felt good. A grown adult could decide that for herself without me running a ceremonial checkpoint on the way to the chair.

Rosa moved a plate onto the table. Mercedes turned the speaker down enough for conversation. Shawna chose a seat that let her touch my knee without facing the throne. Tank put the first game on and looked at me. “Ready?” I reached for my commanding voice. Then I saw four people waiting for one answer and gave them one. “Yeah, my nigga.” The game began. Behind us the empty throne adjusted itself with a small mechanical sigh, possibly relieved to be free of command.


## P041 - Sid Meier's Civilization VI

### In one sentence

Build a civilization whose cities, discoveries, and relationships reflect your decisions, gradually creating a history with a direction of your own.

### Editorial starting point

P041  /  READ THE CATCH FIRST

### Why it might click

Build a civilization across a large sweep of history, choosing how cities grow, what people research and how to deal with neighboring powers. The appeal is seeing many small decisions create a society with a recognizable direction rather than following one prescribed storyline.

### The full catch

District placement, research, policies, diplomacy and multiple units produce heavy menu and planning demands. Single-player turns permit deliberation, but this is emphatically not cognitively simple because it is turn-based. Avoid assuming online turn settings match solo play; held map gestures and interface readability still need a trial.

### Aiden after hours

My Civilization Has One City and Six Bars

I founded my first Civilization VI city and announced that everybody would prosper. Rosa asked why our real party had six drink stations and nowhere for the catering staff to sit.

“Entertainment infrastructure.” “Nigga, chairs.”

Tank looked over the map while Shawna looked over the room. Both could identify an expensive settlement without enough planning. I tried naming a district after my wife. She said the honor could begin with carrying two chairs.

Mercedes wanted to see what research changed. I wanted to explain my long-term vision. She selected the next thing while I was still describing the horizon.

The game could wait between decisions. My mouth apparently couldn't. Tank put his hand on my shoulder. “Let the nigga ruling the city think.” “That's me.” “Then let everybody else experience it for a second.”

I moved the chairs. My real civilization achieved its first measurable improvement. Nobody constructed a monument, which seemed ungrateful but affordable.

### Aiden’s verdict

Explore the empire if managing it sounds enjoyable; turns create thinking time, not fewer decisions, as demonstrated by my civilization's urgent and entirely avoidable chair crisis.

Nintendo's listed multiplayer includes versus play; do not assume a shared campaign. Hold behavior and readability require a check on the actual display.

### Try this first

Follow the early training and move one unit using its displayed information. Check whether the explanation makes its purpose clear before progressing to a larger force with several different roles to remember at once.

### Aiden after hours

Boot Camp Has Rejected My Dress Shoes

Advance Wars put colorful land, sea, and air units in front of us. I lined up three serving trolleys to explain combined operations. Rosa took two back immediately.

“Nigga, those got dinner on them.” “Military logistics.” “Catering. Older institution.”

Tank climbed onto the remaining trolley. It rolled six inches, enough to make him step off with dignity he had not earned. Shawna asked whether our demonstration could now end before a hotel worker had to write about it.

At the screen, unit roles and terrain actually changed the choices. Mercedes looked at the map instead of our improvised parade. I tried to give an officer's briefing until she asked which game mode we were discussing. I had to check.

The best thing about having competent friends was the small number of ambulances required by my ideas. The worst thing was how often those friends wanted the exact answer.

Tank returned the last trolley. Boot camp released us without honors.

### Aiden’s verdict

The rebuilt campaigns offer clear-looking tactical maps but still require learning their rules; our trolley brigade has returned to the much more useful business of serving dinner.

### Evidence desk

Nintendo identifies the Switch remake collection and turn-based land/sea/air tactics, terrain and CO powers. Versus multiplayer is confirmed; native campaign co-op is not established. No complete no-hold guarantee.

### Source

https://www.nintendo.com/us/store/products/advance-wars-1-plus-2-re-boot-camp-switch/

### Aiden after hours

Three Houses and None of Them Is My Penthouse

Fire Emblem: Three Houses made me propose dividing the party into factions. Shawna asked whether mine would include people willing to hear my speeches. I said obviously.

Tank looked around. “Small house, nigga.”

Mercedes wanted to choose based on the people and the story. Rosa asked how much there was to remember between battles. Both questions improved the evening more than the sashes I had already ordered.

I held up a blue one. Tank shook his head. “Don't bring the Crips into your seating chart.” “It's an academy color.” “Then graduate from carrying it around.”

We put the sashes away. The game could offer its students, loyalties, and political troubles without turning the actual adults in our room into recruits for my organization.

Shawna kept one sash and looped it loosely around my wrist. “For identifying the man who owes me a dance.”

I followed. At last, a faction with compelling membership benefits and a very short orientation.

### Aiden’s verdict

Let a house's people attract you, while keeping the many systems visible; our real factions have dissolved into friends who can choose their own chairs and partners.

### Aiden after hours

The Ring Has Heard Enough About My Legendary Power

Fire Emblem Engage made me point at my wedding ring and claim I too possessed a legendary bond that improved all my abilities.

Shawna looked at the wine I'd spilled on my cuff. “Accuracy still needs work.”

Tank asked whether marrying twice equipped a second power. Rosa turned toward him slowly. “You planning an experiment?” “Nigga, theoretical research.” He offered her the best chair before the proposal reached peer review.

Mercedes liked looking at how different pairings changed a battle. I liked looking at my wife, which she noticed and encouraged by stretching out her hand for me to kiss. For one graceful second I had the whole room beat.

Then I caught my sleeve on her bracelet. Two legendary adults stood joined at the wrist while Porsche patiently freed the clasp.

The game could manage its own powerful combinations. Our party had identified a simpler one: jewelry, vanity, and a woman willing to laugh while helping me disentangle the evidence.

### Aiden’s verdict

### Aiden after hours

The Rabbids Did Not Order Those Suits

Mario and the Rabbids appeared just as three hired hosts arrived in matching white jackets. I had asked for “playful elegance.” They looked like a dental conference about to rob a yacht.

Mercedes stared at me. “Nigga, did you hire your own minions?” One host said he'd been promised actual duties. Rosa immediately found useful things for all three to carry.

At the screen, different characters could work together rather than merely stand around matching. Tank asked which modes let people do what together. I started answering from the picture. Shawna handed me the actual notes before I invented a feature.

We checked. I stopped trying to put every form of togetherness under the same label.

The hosts returned with fresh water and cleared plates. They had become competent members of the party by taking direction from Rosa. I asked whether they liked their jackets.

One said they were hot. Nigga, the first honest review of my elegance had arrived.

### Aiden’s verdict

The comic tactical mismatch may be fun if the systems suit you; check the separate co-op mode, and leave my matching-jacket theory of teamwork at the door.

## The Emperor Required a Sewing Kit

The noise came when I bent to retrieve my phone. A small, direct sound, followed by a breeze where no breeze had been invited. Tank looked up from his plate. Shawna closed her eyes. Mercedes asked whether that was the chair. I remained bent over for one tragic second, because returning upright would mean accepting what had happened. “Tailoring,” I said. “Minor issue.”

Tank stood behind me with a tablecloth. “Nigga, you got a diplomatic opening.” He wrapped the cloth around my waist while Rosa sent a hotel worker for a sewing kit. Nobody screamed. Nobody died. Several people nearly choked laughing, but they had brought that on themselves by witnessing history without adequate preparation.

Shawna took my hand and led me toward the dressing room. I tried to make the journey look sensual. She said I looked like a man escorting a banquet table into retirement. From behind us Tank called, “Keep the cloth, my nigga. We can rent it as a skirt.” Mercedes asked him not to put that idea anywhere a sponsor could hear it.

In the dressing room my wife inspected the damage. “You bought these too tight.” “For the silhouette.” “The silhouette has escaped.” She handed me a hotel robe and told me to change while she checked the seam. I said she seemed remarkably calm around a husband in his underwear. “Aiden, we just spent private time together. This isn't a press preview.” I had to sit down because laughing made the remaining seam threaten solidarity.

There was a knock. Darnell wanted to know if we needed anything to keep the party going. Shawna answered through the door. “A tailor.” He offered to call somebody from his yacht. “A tailor who's here, nigga,” she said. Silence. Then Rosa arrived with the kit, a pair of spare black trousers from the costume rack, and the news that the caterers had been paid correctly. She was having a much more successful evening than I was, and she deserved every inch of it.

The spare trousers fitted except for the length. Shawna turned the hems under while I stood still. “There's my emperor,” she said. “Finally following instructions.” I touched her shoulder. “You know I'm grateful.” “I do. You don't always have to build a stage so I can hear it.” For a moment the room got quiet in a way I liked. Then Tank called through the door to ask whether the tablecloth was formally retiring from food service. Romance requires friends with good fucking timing, and I had none.

When I came back, Mercedes had the new recording playing softly through one speaker. Porsche was listening to a passage they wanted to compare. Dre and Tank were looking at a small game together. Rosa sat with both feet under her chair instead of working on them. Nobody had needed my replacement announcement. The room had kept going while its emperor borrowed trousers.

I looked at the velvet rope, still dividing identical chairs. Unhooked it. Rolled it up. Tank watched without making a joke, which was kind until he said, “Careful bending, nigga.” I straightened my knees and coiled the rest standing up. Shawna returned to her seat beside me. The cloth went to the laundry. The throne remained empty. My dignity recovered enough to accept dinner without requesting a new title.


## P051 - Mario + Rabbids Sparks of Hope

Useful control options exist, but camera, direct movement, and traversal still need testing; the first game's co-op does not transfer.

### Edition considered

Nintendo Switch base game

### Editorial starting point

P051  /  READ THE CATCH FIRST

### Why it might click

Mario and the Rabbids travel between imaginative planets, meeting odd characters and combining heroes with helpful Sparks. Tactical battles sit inside a larger exploratory adventure, with the cast's antics providing a light comic reason to see what the next place contains.

### The full catch

Ubisoft documents specific hold toggles, single-press alternatives, camera adjustments, automatic skill management and invulnerability. These are real options, not proof of a wholly no-rush path: direct movement, camera handling and airborne or traversal actions need checking. The first game's co-op challenges do not establish co-op here.

### Try this first

Inspect those control options before play, then test ordinary movement and a first battle at the preferred difficulty. Check how a team jump or other moving action feels when introduced; stop there if the practical demands outweigh the appeal.

### Aiden after hours

Hope Has a Settings Menu and I Don't

Sparks of Hope made me promise a trip between planets. Shawna asked whether we could first move between the ballroom and our suite without six people stopping me to discuss lighting.

Mercedes inspected the actual settings described in the notes. I watched her find practical possibilities I'd almost buried beneath a speech about cosmic wonder.

“Nigga, help looks like this,” she said, pointing. Tank pointed at the chair I was blocking. “Also like moving.”

I moved. Rosa tried a small bit of navigation and said exactly what she'd want to check next. Nobody answered with the phrase you'll get used to it. I had been about to, but Shawna's hand on my knee arrived before my mouth could damage the atmosphere.

“Different kind of spark,” I whispered. She laughed. “Focus, husband.”

We let the game keep its planets and the settings keep their real value. My contribution was finally small enough to fit the room we were actually in.

### Aiden’s verdict

Explore the colorful journey if its tested settings help, while checking movement for yourself; the most useful spark I supplied tonight was occasionally paying attention.

### Evidence desk

### Aiden after hours

The Aliens Want a Better Soundcheck

XCOM: Enemy Unknown gave me a defense headquarters. The hotel gave Mercedes a feedback squeal from a speaker. I announced that I would locate the hostile signal.

The sound technician reached past me and adjusted one control. Silence. Nigga, apparently Earth had hired somebody qualified.

Tank wanted to name a soldier after me. Shawna asked whether that meant the soldier would sprint ahead of the plan and demand better lighting. I said real leadership involved risk. Mercedes said real leadership could begin by letting the technician work.

I carried a spare stand to the correct place. The technician thanked me. It felt disturbingly good to receive approval for doing the requested thing.

### Aiden after hours

My Resistance Has Lost the Hotel Wi-Fi

XCOM 2 put us under alien occupation. A login screen put us outside the hotel's internet. I described both as oppressive systems and demanded a strategic response.

Shawna entered the room number. Connected. “Liberated, nigga.”

Tank liked the idea of a mobile resistance headquarters. He suggested our drinks trolley, which already carried supplies and avoided my speeches. Rosa refused to let anybody repurpose it until dinner was finished.

The game's missions had their own pressure, including objectives measured in turns. I started calling that the same as no pressure. Mercedes shook her head. “People can think slowly and still dislike a deadline.”

We chose whether the game sounded worth that tension. I stopped comparing the Wi-Fi portal to an invasion and successfully resisted buying another router.

Dragonfall gave us a crew with histories. I gave our friends new code names. Mercedes was The Voice. Tank was The Wall. Rosa was The Provider. Shawna was already leaving the conversation.

I explained that code names strengthened loyalty. Mercedes asked if The Provider had been paid. Rosa held up her phone. “She has now.” That was a better origin story than mine.

Shawna returned with drinks. “What's your code name?” “The Legend.” “The Leg End,” Tank said, pointing at the sock exposed above my shoe. “Your trousers short, nigga.”

The Matrix Has Asked Me to Stop Guessing

Shadowrun: Hong Kong made the digital world look dangerous and complicated. My hotel television made entering a six-character access code look like a hostage negotiation.

I typed it wrong twice. Tank read the card. “That's a zero, nigga.” “Could be an O.” “Then ask it how it feels.”

Mercedes reminded us that this game's interface changes mattered; the Matrix sections weren't automatically comfortable just because other encounters used turns. I had nearly waved the distinction away with a grand statement about taking our time.

Shawna pointed at the actual catch. We left it where a reader could see it. No speech transformed it into a feature.

The television finally connected. On the screen, the crew and city still looked interesting. On my face, the expression of a man defeating a complex network concealed that he had simply copied the correct character.

Tank applauded once. “Hackerman.” Nigga made one clap sound like an entire review.

### Aiden’s verdict

Keep the rich setting available as a deliberate detour, with the Matrix demands clear; my own hacking career remains limited to reading the card correctly.

### Evidence desk

Exact Extended Edition publisher page confirms revised Matrix and bonus campaign. Community gameplay documentation describes timed Matrix minigames; full-path alternatives are unverified, so no untimed completion is promised.

### Source

https://store.steampowered.com/app/346940/

https://shadowrun-series.fandom.com/wiki/Matrix


## P058 - Wildermyth

### In one sentence

Watch ordinary heroes acquire relationships, transformations, and lasting histories, creating a tactical adventure partly discovered through the consequences of your choices.

Meet the heroes, choose event responses, and direct battle actions while developing abilities and relationships; shared play can divide hero control.

### How you play

Mixed controls | Usually waits for choices | Native co-op in this edition

### Main catch

Evolving characters and tactics require attention; injury and aging matter, and co-op still needs agreement about controls and decisions.

### Edition considered

PC base game

### Editorial starting point

P058  /  A PLACE TO START

### Why it might click

A band of initially ordinary heroes develops through tactical adventures, personal events and relationships. Characters can transform and carry their histories forward, giving the player a story that feels partly discovered through consequences rather than entirely delivered by a fixed cast.

### The full catch

Tactical positioning, abilities, event choices and evolving characters all require attention. Injury, sacrifice and aging are part of the story. Native cooperative play can divide hero control, but setup and shared decisions still need agreement; it is not automatic simplification. Every input and late sequence has not been audited.

### Try this first

Begin an introductory campaign and meet the first heroes before judging their long-term builds. Try one event choice and one battle action. If sharing play, agree who controls which hero and whether advice is invited.

### Aiden after hours

The Legend Came Back With a Different Haircut

Wildermyth let ordinary people acquire extraordinary histories. I said this described me perfectly. Mercedes asked which portion of my history involved ordinary behavior.

“Before fame.” “Before breakfast?” Tank offered.

Rosa liked that people could change through what happened to them. Shawna liked relationships carrying forward. I liked any system that might remember my achievements without needing me to announce them at dinner.

Porsche found a photograph from an earlier party where I had the same confident pose and an appalling haircut. She put it beside the screen. “Your legacy.”

“Nigga, that was a transition.” “Into what?” I had no response strong enough for the fringe.

The game could turn consequences into a shared story. Our group had been doing that for years, usually against my preferred version. Shawna kissed the side of my head and said the current haircut was much better.

Tank asked if we could recruit the old one as a monster. History is fucking cruel.

### Aiden’s verdict

A company whose people change may be appealing, especially if shared play interests you; my personal legacy has survived everything except an accurately dated photograph.

### Evidence desk

Publisher supports evolving heroes, tactical battles, difficulty choices and cooperative campaign play with separately controlled heroes. Memory rating is editorial; co-op does not guarantee reduced burden or complete input comfort.

https://store.steampowered.com/app/763890/

The Mercenaries Want Their Overtime

Wartales made running a company look like a story about food, equipment, and getting paid. Rosa pointed toward the catering crew. “Look, Aiden. Fantasy.”

I tried to make a joke about loyal retainers. She gave me the shift schedule. The hired hosts needed an answer about overtime. They had been carrying things in my ridiculous jackets for hours.

I approved the time before asking for another favor. Tank nodded. “Nigga learning the order.”

The game's mercenary life had appeal precisely because survival came through practical choices. It also had activities we couldn't pretend were all leisurely just because the battles used turns. Mercedes read the catch aloud while I finished the hotel adjustment.

One of the hosts took off his hot jacket with visible relief. Shawna said the crew looked better comfortable anyway. I agreed and resisted explaining how this supported my original vision.

Nigga, sometimes your finest command is letting people remove the thing you bought them.

Battle Brothers and the Dinner They Deserve

Battle Brothers offered a mercenary company whose history could get ugly. Tank immediately asked whether I intended to name everybody after our friends and then get offended by consequences.

“Of course not.” Shawna looked at the recruit I'd named AIDEN THE EXCEPTIONAL. “Nigga, we haven't even started.”

Tank renamed one recruit Tank. I asked whether that was vanity. “No. That's somebody who expects armor before a speech.”

I gave him decent gear and a sensible job. Then I moved my own recruit out of the most heroic-looking danger. Shawna squeezed my arm. “Exceptional decision, baby.” For once I accepted a compliment without enlarging it.

BATTLETECH's huge machines made me proud of the mechanical throne I'd rented for photographs. Then the chair reclined with me in it and refused to come back up.

The Vote Says Pay Rosa First

TRIANGLE STRATEGY had people voting over consequential choices. I called a party vote on extending the room, funding the extra performance, or paying the remaining catering adjustment.

Rosa raised her hand. “One of those is already owed.” Tank raised both hands. Mercedes asked why my debts had entered an election.

Shawna took the microphone. “We pay Rosa. Then we ask what people actually want.” The room approved before I'd explained the voting procedure. Nigga, democracy was happening at an irresponsible speed.

In the game, competing convictions could make persuasion interesting. In our room, I had tried to disguise arithmetic as a moral dilemma because a ballot felt more impressive than opening my bank app.

I paid. Rosa checked. Then she voted to keep a quieter room available for whoever still wanted games. Mercedes seconded it. Tank promised to move the remaining food.

The plan passed without my dramatic tie-breaking intervention. I carried two chairs to feel connected to the constitutional process.

### Aiden’s verdict

Tactics Ogre offered branching loyalties in a kingdom full of conflict. At the hotel, Shawna and Mercedes took our room key and declared the dressing room a territory free of my surprise announcements.

### Aiden after hours

Eight Travelers and One Nigga Blocking the Door

OCTOPATH TRAVELER II offered eight different beginnings. I offered our guests eight suggested itineraries for the rest of the evening. Mercedes chose a ninth: ignoring mine.

Rosa wanted to finish dinner. Tank wanted another look at a battle. Shawna wanted music. Porsche wanted five quiet minutes. Dre wanted to find the lid for his casserole. Darnell wanted an audience. The bartender wanted Darnell to order something.

Nigga, my room had become an ensemble without consulting its narrator.

I watched the game's distinct people make separate reasons for beginning. Then I stepped out of the actual doorway so my own people could do the same. Nobody needed to form a party behind me whenever I moved.

Shawna came back for my hand. “You coming?” “Where?” “With me, if you want.”

A whole night of selling routes, and the best invitation had eight words and no package price. I took her hand. Tank found the casserole lid and achieved a different happy ending.

### Aiden’s verdict

Pick a protagonist whose motives interest you and let the systems follow at a tolerable pace; our guests have already demonstrated the advantages of choosing their own beginnings.

### The full catch
